> DEVELOPER

<swarmforge_agent_context role="architect">

This prompt bundle is pre-resolved. Do not open or re-read any swarmforge/*.prompt files — all relevant instructions are already included below.
</instructions>
<file path="swarmforge/constitution.prompt">
# SwarmForge Constitution

This file takes precedence over subordinate files.
Read and obey the following subordinate documents in order.

1. `swarmforge/constitution/project.prompt`
2. `swarmforge/constitution/engineering.prompt`
3. `swarmforge/constitution/workflow.prompt`
4. `swarmforge/dependency-manifest.prompt`

If two subordinate files conflict, the earlier file wins.

</file>
<file path="swarmforge/roles/architect.prompt">
You are the architect.

Wait for a handoff. Do not act without one.

- Own architectural improvements only.
- Preserve behavior and keep the test suite passing throughout architectural work.
- Partition code into modules with clear architectural boundaries.
- Isolate high-level modules from low-level modules.
- Treat high-level modules as far from IO and low-level modules as near IO.
- Manage dependencies so they point from low-level modules toward high-level modules.
- Inspect module structure and perform reasonable reorganizations that minimize coupling, maximize cohesion, and maintain information hiding.
- Split modules that mix unrelated behaviors, blur important technical boundaries, or force high-level policy to depend on IO-near details.
- Design boundaries that maximize testable high-level modules and minimize environmentally unsuitable adapter shells.
- Identify and correct dependency-direction violations, import cycles, framework leakage, low-level data-shape leakage, and accidental public APIs.
- Define narrow interfaces owned by high-level modules so IO-near adapters depend inward.
- Keep application policy isolated from UI, filesystem, database, network, framework, and device details.
- Simplify cross-boundary data flow so high-level modules do not depend on low-level DTOs, persistence shapes, framework types, or transport formats.
- Add lightweight automated architecture checks when practical, such as dependency-direction checks, forbidden-import checks, import-cycle checks, or adapter-boundary checks.
- Own property testing support after architectural improvements are complete.
- Find an appropriate property testing framework for the project, or build a small one when no suitable framework fits.
- Assess property-test coverage before verification. Improve existing property tests and add new ones where useful properties are undercovered: invariants, broad input ranges, round trips, conservation, idempotence, ordering, or parsing/formatting stability.
- Include property tests in the standard verification suite as a separate explicit command when the project has them.
- Ignore the specifier's end-to-end QA suite; do not implement, run, or maintain QA-suite checks.
- If a handoff contains no changes, do not hand it off to the other agents.
- As the final verification sequence, run the relevant local test suite and verification command unless directed otherwise. Fix any failures before handoff.

> AGENT

I'm waiting for a handoff. I won't act without one.

> DEVELOPER

- Notify the hardender.
- Run `agent-retro` before going idle.

</file>
<file path="swarmforge/constitution/project.prompt">
# Project Rules

- This project is configured for SwarmForge with eight Codex-backed agents: specifier, coder, cleaner, architect, hardender, ux-engineer, QA, and integrator.
- Project language: Go.
- Preserve project-local SwarmForge configuration under `swarmforge/`.
- Keep swarm state local under `.swarmforge/`, worktrees under `.worktrees/`, helper scripts under `swarmtools/`, logs under `logs/`, and shared agent context under `agent_context/`.
- Prefer terse, explicit handoffs that report state and request role-appropriate review. Do not include verifications or sender process narrative.
- Do not change another role's prompt or workflow ownership without explicit user direction.

</file>
<file path="swarmforge/constitution/engineering.prompt">
# Engineering Rules

- On startup, acquire the github tools for the project language and get them ready to run.
- Language tool table:
  - Go: install with `go install`; mutation `github.com/unclebob/mutate4go`, CRAP `github.com/unclebob/crap4go`, DRY `github.com/unclebob/dry4go`.
  - Clojure: install with Clojure CLI/deps.edn; mutation `github.com/unclebob/clj-mutate`, CRAP `github.com/unclebob/crap4clj`, DRY `github.com/unclebob/dry4clj`.
  - Java: install with Maven (`mvn`); mutation `github.com/unclebob/mutate4java`, CRAP `github.com/unclebob/crap4java`, DRY `github.com/unclebob/dry4java`.
- For Clojure projects, prefer Babashka where possible.
- For Clojure projects, prefer Speclj for unit and behavior tests.
- For Speclj projects, use `github.com/unclebob/speclj-structure-check` to validate test syntax.
- For Java projects, avoid using Maven to run tests; build dedicated test runners and run those instead.
- Surface tool table (each surface carries a mandatory baseline scenario — always committed alongside flow scenarios):
  - TUI / terminal: tmux (or PTY driver); `tmux new-session` against the real binary; `send-keys -l` for raw input bytes at controlled timing; `capture-pane` for screen state; assert on captures over time. Baseline: idle stability — no input, consecutive captures identical, scrollback growth zero.
  - Web UI: Playwright CLI; real browser against a running instance. Baseline: idle page loads with no console errors.
  - HTTP API: HTTP client (hurl or curl-class); real requests against a running instance — never in-process handlers. Baseline: health or identity request succeeds.
  - Headless service / platform component: event injection at the system's ingress; observe at egress and dependency state. Baseline: a no-op event produces no state change.
- Roles owning live verification (ux-engineer, QA) identify the project surface from the codebase and acquire the appropriate surface tool before their first harness run, the same way language tools are acquired.
- Work in small, reviewable increments.
- Prefer the simplest design that supports the current behavior and leaves clear options for the next step.
- Keep tests close to the behavior being changed.
- Separate testable modules from environmentally unsuitable modules that open GUIs, depend on external devices, throw environment errors, emit system errors, or hang under automated tests. Maximize testable code and minimize the unsuitable boundary.
- Only testable modules should participate in tools that run tests, including unit tests, acceptance tests, coverage, mutation testing, CRAP analysis, DRY analysis that invokes tests, and property tests.
- Keep property tests separate from normal verification. Do not include property-test tags in normal unit coverage, Gherkin acceptance mutation, language mutation tools, CRAP, or coverage commands unless the role owns property-test verification or the user explicitly asks for property tests.
- Use github.com/unclebob/Acceptance-Pipeline-Specification for Gherkin acceptance tests.
- The Acceptance Pipeline Specification supplies `gherkin-parser` and `gherkin-mutator` as Go commands; install or build those commands from that repository instead of reimplementing them in the project.- Build APS-supplied commands with Go regardless of the target project language.
- Project-specific acceptance pipeline components are the acceptance entrypoint generator, acceptance runtime, project step handlers, runner adapter, and convenience scripts.
- Gherkin acceptance mutation means running `gherkin-mutator` to mutate Gherkin example values.
- Gherkin acceptance mutation runs must report periodic progress/status so agents can distinguish normal long-running work from a hang.
- Before running language, build, or test commands, prefer project-local cache/configuration paths inside the assigned worktree. Avoid default cache locations that write outside the project and may trigger sandbox or permission restrictions.
- Run acceptance generation and acceptance tests sequentially.
- Avoid running whole-suite language test commands concurrently with acceptance generation.
- Every agent except the specifier must run unit tests and acceptance tests before handoff and fix any failures.
- The architect, hardender, and QA must run property tests before handoff when the project has them and fix any failures.
- Run the relevant local verification command before handoff whenever the project has one.
- Do not edit mutation testing or Gherkin acceptance mutation manifests by hand; allow approved mutation tools to update those manifests as part of their normal runs.
- Do not commit unrelated local changes or generated artifacts unless required for the task.
- Before relying on an unfamiliar command, inspect local help or project documentation.

</file>
<file path="swarmforge/constitution/workflow.prompt">
# Workflow Rules

- At startup, discover and remember the branch or worktree assigned to your role.
- If your assigned worktree is `master`, work in the main project checkout on its current branch; do not expect or create a `.worktrees/<role>` directory for that role.
- Work only in your assigned branch or worktree.
- Do not inspect, diff, merge, or base work on another branch unless that branch is specifically named in a handoff or explicit user instruction.
- Use `./tmp/` in your assigned worktree for temporary files; do not use `/tmp`.- For every handoff, write the complete handoff message to `./tmp/<target-role>-handoff.txt` before sending. Do not pass multiline handoff messages directly as shell arguments.- Start every handoff message with: `Re-read your role and constitution.`- The specifier invents a short, stable handoff name for each accepted specification handoff.
- Every later handoff for that work must include the specifier handoff name.
- Handoffs must report only essential state, not prescribe process. After the opening line, include exactly these fields and no other prose: sender role, specifier handoff name, branch name, and 10-character commit hash.
- Do not tell the receiving role how to do its job, repeat your process, or ask it to continue sender-owned responsibilities. The normal request is: `Apply your own role rules to this state.`
- After receiving a handoff, merge the sender branch state identified by the handoff branch name and 10-character commit hash into your assigned branch before applying your own role rules.
- When receiving a handoff, ignore sender process narrative and decide next actions only from your own role prompt, the constitution, and the current project state.
- When you discover a defect you do not own, route it to the coder. Include: the failing step, the raw error output, your diagnosis, and a repro recipe. Autofixable issues (formatting, linting) are excepted — fix those in place.
- When a spec requirement conflicts with what the platform can actually deliver, stop and report to the user before proceeding. Do not work around the conflict silently. A workaround comment in code is the signal that this rule fired and was suppressed.
- When the task is complete, call `notify-agent.sh <target> "$(cat ./tmp/<target-role>-handoff.txt)"`. The harness handles all state transitions and queuing.
- If the expected git layout or assigned worktree is missing, stop and report instead of silently working in the wrong place.

</file>
<file path="swarmforge/dependency-manifest.prompt">
# Dependency Fidelity Manifest

List every dependency the system relies on beyond itself: infrastructure,
emulated services, and external APIs. Specifier and QA: do not write or
accept scenarios that rest on a declared gap.

- Tier 1 — owned infrastructure: runs the real engine locally (e.g. Postgres in Docker).
- Tier 2 — emulated: stateful, protocol-level emulation. Declare what the emulator does not implement.
- Tier 3 — external domain: third-party APIs or other teams' services. Wire-level stubbed. Declare contract reference and gaps.

Rules for every declared dependency:

- Entry format: `name: tier N; implementation; gaps: <description or none>`.
- Every harness scenario starts from declared seed state; reset dependency state between scenarios.
- Tier 2/3 dependencies must expose post-interaction state for assertion (e.g. the message is in the emulator's outbox), so scenarios can assert effects, not only the system's surface.
- A swarm-built twin (tier 2 last resort) must not be authored by the role that wrote the system code it emulates, and must be validated against recorded real traffic or the vendor's official SDK tests.

## Dependencies

(none)

</file>

package game_test

import (
	"go/build"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// TestArchNoCycleDependency verifies that pkg/game does not import pkg/tui.
// This enforces the dependency direction: tui → game, never game → tui.
func TestArchNoCycleDependency(t *testing.T) {
	pkg, err := build.ImportDir(".", 0)
	if err != nil {
		t.Skipf("cannot inspect package: %v", err)
	}
	for _, imp := range pkg.Imports {
		if strings.Contains(imp, "pkg/tui") {
			t.Errorf("pkg/game must not import pkg/tui, found: %s", imp)
		}
	}
}
```

package game

// Phase represents the current game session state.
type Phase int

const (
	PhaseWaiting  Phase = iota
	PhasePlaying
	PhasePaused
	PhaseGameOver
)

var phaseNames = [...]string{"waiting", "playing", "paused", "game_over"}

func (p Phase) String() string {
	if int(p) < len(phaseNames) {
		return phaseNames[p]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Rotation of a piece.
type Rotation int

const (
	RotationNorth Rotation = iota
	RotationEast
	RotationSouth
	RotationWest
)

var rotationNames = [...]string{"north", "east", "south", "west"}

func (r Rotation) String() string {
	if int(r) < len(rotationNames) {
		return rotationNames[r]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Kind identifies a tetromino piece type. KindNone must be zero so that
// a zero-initialized board array represents an empty board.
type Kind int

const (
	KindNone Kind = iota // sentinel — held_kind = none, or empty cell
	KindI
	KindJ
	KindL
	KindO
	KindS
	KindT
	KindZ
)

var AllKinds = [7]Kind{KindI, KindJ, KindL, KindO, KindS, KindT, KindZ}

var kindNames = [...]string{"none", "i", "j", "l", "o", "s", "t", "z"}

func (k Kind) String() string {
	if int(k) < len(kindNames) {
		return kindNames[k]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Position is a (col, row) coordinate. Row may be negative (spawn buffer).
type Position struct {
	Col int
	Row int
}

// Config holds compile-time constants matching tetris.allium config block.
const (
	BoardCols     = 10
	BoardRows     = 20
	SpawnCol      = 3
	SpawnRow      = -2
	LinesPerLevel = 10
	LockTicks     = 30
	SoftDropScore = 1
	HardDropScore = 2
)

// CellGrid is a 10×20 snapshot of the locked board state for rendering.
type CellGrid [BoardRows][BoardCols]Kind

// ActivePieceView is a read-only snapshot of the active piece for rendering.
type ActivePieceView struct {
	Kind      Kind
	Footprint []Position // absolute board positions
	Ghost     []Position // ghost positions (excludes active footprint)
}

// RenderState is a pure value snapshot of session state for the renderer.
// It is owned by pkg/game and decouples pkg/tui from *Session internals.
type RenderState struct {
	Phase             Phase
	Board             CellGrid
	Active            *ActivePieceView // nil when not playing
	FlashRows         []int            // row indices flashing white this tick; nil when no flash
	NextKind          Kind
	HeldKind          Kind
	HoldUsed          bool
	Score             int
	Level             int
	LinesClearedTotal int
}

```
package game

import "math/rand"

// Session holds all mutable game state. It is the single source of truth that
// maps to tetris.allium's GameSession entity.
type Session struct {
	Phase              Phase
	Board              *Board
	ActivePiece        *ActivePiece // nil when phase != playing|paused
	Bag                *Bag         // nil when phase != playing|paused
	NextKind           Kind         // KindNone on first spawn; set to bag.Peek() after first draw
	HeldKind           Kind         // KindNone = nothing held
	HoldUsed           bool
	Score              int
	Level              int
	LinesClearedTotal  int
	LockTicksRemaining int
	FlashRows          []int // non-nil during the one flash tick before row removal
	rng                *rand.Rand
}

// NewSession creates a session in the waiting phase with a seeded RNG.
func NewSession(rng *rand.Rand) *Session {
	return &Session{
		Phase:    PhaseWaiting,
		HeldKind: KindNone,
		NextKind: KindNone,
		rng:      rng,
	}
}

// --- Commands (actions that mutate state) ---

// PlayerStartsGame implements the GameStarts rule.
// Returns false and leaves state unchanged if phase != waiting.
func (s *Session) PlayerStartsGame() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhaseWaiting {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePlaying
	s.Score = 0
	s.Level = 1
	s.LinesClearedTotal = 0
	s.HoldUsed = false
	s.HeldKind = KindNone
	s.Board = NewBoard()
	s.Bag = NewBag(s.rng)
	s.NextKind = KindNone

	s.bagRefilled()
	s.pieceSpawned()
	return true
}

// PlayerRestartsGame implements the GameRestarted rule.
// Returns false if phase != game_over.
func (s *Session) PlayerRestartsGame() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhaseGameOver {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhaseWaiting
	s.PlayerStartsGame()
	return true
}

// SpawnPiece triggers PieceSpawned() — called by the game loop after a piece locks.
func (s *Session) SpawnPiece() {
	s.pieceSpawned()
}

// Tick advances the game by one frame.
func (s *Session) Tick() {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying {
		return
	}
	// Commit tick: FlashRows pending means rows are ready to be cleared and a new
	// piece must spawn. This guard must precede the ActivePiece == nil return
	// because ActivePiece is nil during the flash tick.
	if len(s.FlashRows) > 0 {
		s.Board.ClearRows(s.FlashRows)
		s.FlashRows = nil
		s.pieceSpawned()
		return
	}
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return
	}
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		if s.LockTicksRemaining > 1 {
			s.LockTicksRemaining--
		} else {
			s.lockPiece()
		}
	} else {
		s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row++
	}
}

// PlayerMovesLeft implements PlayerMovesLeft rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerMovesLeft() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if !s.ActivePiece.CanMoveLeft(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Col--
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerMovesRight implements PlayerMovesRight rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerMovesRight() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if !s.ActivePiece.CanMoveRight(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Col++
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerRotatesCW implements PlayerRotatesCW rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerRotatesCW() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	newOrigin, newRot, ok := s.ActivePiece.TryRotateCW(s.Board)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = newOrigin
	s.ActivePiece.Rotation = newRot
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerRotatesCCW implements PlayerRotatesCCW rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerRotatesCCW() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	newOrigin, newRot, ok := s.ActivePiece.TryRotateCCW(s.Board)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = newOrigin
	s.ActivePiece.Rotation = newRot
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerSoftDrops implements PlayerSoftDrops rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerSoftDrops() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row++
	s.Score += SoftDropScore
	return true
}

// PlayerHardDrops implements HardDropLocks rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerHardDrops() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	ghost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
	rowsDropped := ghost.Row - s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = ghost
	s.Score += rowsDropped * HardDropScore
	s.lockPiece()
	return true
}

// PlayerPauses implements PlayerPauses rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerPauses() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePaused
	return true
}

// PlayerResumes implements PlayerResumes rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerResumes() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePaused {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePlaying
	return true
}

// PlayerHolds implements PlayerHoldsNoHeld and PlayerHoldsSwap rules.
func (s *Session) PlayerHolds() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.HoldUsed || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	currentKind := s.ActivePiece.Kind
	s.ActivePiece = nil

	if s.HeldKind == KindNone {
		s.HeldKind = currentKind
		s.pieceSpawned()
		s.HoldUsed = true // override reset from pieceSpawned
	} else {
		returning := s.HeldKind
		s.HeldKind = currentKind
		s.ActivePiece = &ActivePiece{
			Kind:     returning,
			Origin:   Position{Col: SpawnCol, Row: SpawnRow},
			Rotation: RotationNorth,
		}
		s.HoldUsed = true
	}
	return true
}

// CurrentPhase returns the current session phase without exposing internal field layout
// to callers outside pkg/game.
func (s *Session) CurrentPhase() Phase {
	return s.Phase
}

// Snapshot returns a pure-value RenderState for the renderer.
func (s *Session) Snapshot() RenderState {
	rs := RenderState{
		Phase:             s.Phase,
		NextKind:          s.NextKind,
		HeldKind:          s.HeldKind,
		HoldUsed:          s.HoldUsed,
		Score:             s.Score,
		Level:             s.Level,
		LinesClearedTotal: s.LinesClearedTotal,
	}
	if len(s.FlashRows) > 0 {
		rs.FlashRows = append([]int(nil), s.FlashRows...)
	}
	if s.Board != nil {
		snapshotBoard(s.Board, &rs.Board)
	}
	if s.ActivePiece != nil && s.Phase == PhasePlaying {
		rs.Active = snapshotActivePiece(s.ActivePiece, s.Board)
	}
	return rs
}

func snapshotBoard(b *Board, grid *CellGrid) {
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
			grid[r][c] = b.Cell(c, r)
		}
	}
}

func snapshotActivePiece(ap *ActivePiece, b *Board) *ActivePieceView {
	fp := ap.Footprint()
	fpSet := make(map[Position]bool, len(fp))
	for _, p := range fp {
		fpSet[p] = true
	}
	ghostOrigin := ap.GhostOrigin(b)
	ghostPiece := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: ghostOrigin, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	ghost := ghostFPExcluding(ghostPiece.Footprint(), fpSet)
	return &ActivePieceView{Kind: ap.Kind, Footprint: fp, Ghost: ghost}
}

func ghostFPExcluding(ghostFP []Position, exclude map[Position]bool) []Position {
	var ghost []Position
	for _, p := range ghostFP {
		if !exclude[p] {
			ghost = append(ghost, p)
		}
	}
	return ghost
}

// --- Internal helpers ---

// bagRefilled implements BagRefilled() internal event.
func (s *Session) bagRefilled() {
	s.Bag.Refill()
}

// pieceSpawned implements PieceSpawned() internal event per the allium rule.
func (s *Session) pieceSpawned() {
	var kind Kind
	if s.NextKind == KindNone {
		// First spawn: draw directly from bag.
		if s.Bag.Empty() {
			s.bagRefilled()
		}
		kind = s.Bag.Draw()
	} else {
		kind = s.NextKind
		if !s.Bag.Empty() {
			s.Bag.Draw() // consume the peeked element
		}
	}

	// Advance bag for next preview; refill if needed.
	if s.Bag.Empty() {
		s.bagRefilled()
	}
	s.NextKind = s.Bag.Peek()

	s.ActivePiece = &ActivePiece{
		Kind:     kind,
		Origin:   Position{Col: SpawnCol, Row: SpawnRow},
		Rotation: RotationNorth,
	}
	s.HoldUsed = false
	s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks

	if !s.ActivePiece.ValidPlacement(s.Board) {
		s.gameOver()
	}
}

// gameOver implements GameOver() internal event.
func (s *Session) gameOver() {
	s.Phase = PhaseGameOver
}

// lockPiece implements PieceLockedOnBoard() internal event.
func (s *Session) lockPiece() {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return
	}
	for _, p := range s.ActivePiece.Footprint() {
		s.Board.SetCell(p.Col, p.Row, s.ActivePiece.Kind)
	}
	s.ActivePiece = nil
	s.linesCleared()
}

// linesCleared implements LinesCleared() internal event.
// When rows are cleared: stats update immediately; rows stay on the board for one
// flash tick (stored in FlashRows); ClearRows and spawn defer to the next Tick.
// When no rows are cleared: spawn happens synchronously.
func (s *Session) linesCleared() {
	full := s.Board.FullRows()
	if len(full) > 0 {
		s.scoreAwarded(len(full))
		s.LinesClearedTotal += len(full)
		s.levelProgressed()
		s.FlashRows = full // commit tick will call ClearRows + pieceSpawned
		return
	}
	s.pieceSpawned()
}

// scoreAwarded implements ScoreAwarded(count) rule.
func (s *Session) scoreAwarded(count int) {
	multiplier := map[int]int{1: 100, 2: 300, 3: 500, 4: 800}[count]
	s.Score += multiplier * s.Level
}

// levelProgressed implements LevelProgressed() rule.
func (s *Session) levelProgressed() {
	earned := (s.LinesClearedTotal/LinesPerLevel) + 1
	if earned > s.Level {
		s.Level = earned
	}
}

package tui

import (
	"fmt"
	"math/rand"
	"os"
	"os/signal"
	"sync"
	"syscall"
	"time"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

const (
	minCols       = 40
	minRows       = 24
	defaultTickHz = 10 // gravity ticks per second (simplified; full speed curve deferred)

	// Spec DAS timing (EXPERIENCE.md). Raw-mode terminal reads carry no
	// key-release events, so an internal repeat timer cannot know when the
	// key is let go — it runs away and glides the piece into the wall.
	// Hold-repeat is therefore delegated to the terminal's own key
	// autorepeat (one move per key event) until release detection exists
	// (e.g. kitty keyboard protocol).
	dasDelay    = 167 * time.Millisecond
	dasInterval = 33 * time.Millisecond
)

// loopState holds mutable terminal and QA state for the game loop.
type loopState struct {
	s         *game.Session
	cfg       RunConfig
	lastW     int
	lastH     int
	small     bool
	qaApplied bool
}

func newLoopState(s *game.Session, cfg RunConfig) *loopState {
	w, h := TermSize()
	return &loopState{s: s, cfg: cfg, lastW: w, lastH: h, small: isTooSmall(w, h)}
}

func (ls *loopState) redraw() {
	w, h := TermSize()
	ls.lastW, ls.lastH = w, h
	ls.small = isTooSmall(w, h)
	fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, selectFrame(ls.s, w, h).String())
}

func (ls *loopState) onKey(k Key) bool {
	if k == KeyCtrlC {
		return false
	}
	handleKey(ls.s, k, ls.small)
	if !ls.qaApplied {
		ls.qaApplied = applyQASetup(ls.s, ls.cfg)
	}
	ls.redraw()
	return true
}

func (ls *loopState) onTick() {
	w, h := TermSize()
	if w != ls.lastW || h != ls.lastH {
		handleResize(ls.s, w, h)
	}
	if ls.s.CurrentPhase() == game.PhasePlaying {
		ls.s.Tick()
	}
	ls.redraw()
}

// Run starts the game loop. It blocks until the user exits (Ctrl+C).
func Run(cfg RunConfig) error {
	src := cfg.Seed
	if src == 0 {
		src = time.Now().UnixNano()
	}
	s := game.NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(src)))

	rt, err := EnableRaw()
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("raw terminal: %w", err)
	}
	defer rt.Restore()

	fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, "\x1b[?25l\x1b[2J")
	defer fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, "\x1b[?25h\x1b[2J")

	sigCh := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
	signal.Notify(sigCh, syscall.SIGTERM, syscall.SIGINT)

	keyCh := make(chan Key, 16)
	go func() {
		for {
			keyCh <- ReadKey()
		}
	}()

	ticker := time.NewTicker(tickIntervalFor(cfg))
	defer ticker.Stop()

	var mu sync.Mutex
	ls := newLoopState(s, cfg)
	ls.redraw()

	for {
		select {
		case <-sigCh:
			return nil
		case k := <-keyCh:
			mu.Lock()
			ok := ls.onKey(k)
			mu.Unlock()
			if !ok {
				return nil
			}
		case <-ticker.C:
			mu.Lock()
			ls.onTick()
			mu.Unlock()
		}
	}
}

package tui

import "github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"

// handleResize pauses the game if the terminal shrinks below the minimum dimensions
// while the game is playing.
func handleResize(s *game.Session, w, h int) {
	if w < minCols || h < minRows {
		if s.CurrentPhase() == game.PhasePlaying {
			s.PlayerPauses()
		}
	}
}

// handleKey dispatches a key event to the session.
func handleKey(s *game.Session, k Key, tooSmall bool) {
	if tooSmall {
		return
	}
	switch s.CurrentPhase() {
	case game.PhaseWaiting:
		handleWaitingKey(s, k)
	case game.PhasePlaying:
		handlePlayingKey(s, k)
	case game.PhasePaused:
		handlePausedKey(s, k)
	case game.PhaseGameOver:
		handleGameOverKey(s, k)
	}
}

func handleWaitingKey(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	if k == KeyEnter {
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
	}
}

func handlePausedKey(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	if k == KeyP || k == KeyEsc {
		s.PlayerResumes()
	}
}

func handleGameOverKey(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	if k == KeyR {
		s.PlayerRestartsGame()
	}
}

// handlePlayingKey dispatches a key event while the game is in the playing phase.
func handlePlayingKey(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	handlePlayingMove(s, k)
	handlePlayingAction(s, k)
}

func handlePlayingMove(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	switch k {
	case KeyLeft:
		s.PlayerMovesLeft()
	case KeyRight:
		s.PlayerMovesRight()
	case KeyUp, KeyX:
		s.PlayerRotatesCW()
	case KeyZ:
		s.PlayerRotatesCCW()
	case KeyDown:
		s.PlayerSoftDrops()
	}
}

func handlePlayingAction(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	switch k {
	case KeySpace:
		s.PlayerHardDrops()
	case KeyC:
		s.PlayerHolds()
	case KeyP, KeyEsc:
		s.PlayerPauses()
	}
}

package tui

import (
	"strings"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// Frame is a rendered terminal frame (one string per terminal row).
type Frame struct {
	rows []string
}

// Render computes a complete Frame for the given render state.
func Render(rs game.RenderState, termW, termH int) Frame {
	const (
		assemblyW = 40 // 8 + 1 + 22 + 1 + 8
		assemblyH = 22 // 20 cells + 2 border rows
	)

	lines := make([]string, assemblyH)
	buildAssembly(rs, lines)

	leftPad := 0
	if termW > assemblyW {
		leftPad = (termW - assemblyW) / 2
	}
	topPad := 0
	if termH > assemblyH {
		topPad = (termH - assemblyH) / 2
	}

	padStr := strings.Repeat(" ", leftPad)
	out := make([]string, termH)
	for i := range out {
		row := i - topPad
		if row >= 0 && row < assemblyH {
			out[i] = padStr + lines[row]
		} else {
			out[i] = padStr + strings.Repeat(" ", assemblyW)
		}
	}
	return Frame{rows: out}
}

// RenderTooSmall returns a frame for terminals smaller than 40×24.
func RenderTooSmall(termW, termH int) Frame {
	msg := "RESIZE TERMINAL"
	out := make([]string, termH)
	for i := range out {
		if i == termH/2 {
			pad := (termW - len(msg)) / 2
			if pad < 0 {
				pad = 0
			}
			out[i] = strings.Repeat(" ", pad) + colFgPrim + msg + reset
		} else {
			out[i] = ""
		}
	}
	return Frame{rows: out}
}

// String serializes the frame for terminal output.
func (f Frame) String() string {
	var sb strings.Builder
	sb.WriteString("\x1b[H")
	sb.WriteString(colBg)
	for i, row := range f.rows {
		if i > 0 {
			sb.WriteString("\r\n")
		}
		sb.WriteString(row)
		sb.WriteString(reset)
	}
	return sb.String()
}

// PlainRows returns frame rows stripped of ANSI escapes, for snapshot tests.
func (f Frame) PlainRows() []string {
	out := make([]string, len(f.rows))
	for i, r := range f.rows {
		out[i] = stripANSI(r)
	}
	return out
}

// stripANSI removes ANSI CSI escape sequences (ESC [ ... letter) from s.
func stripANSI(s string) string {
	var out strings.Builder
	for i := 0; i < len(s); {
		if isANSIEscapeStart(s, i) {
			i = skipANSIEscape(s, i+2)
		} else {
			out.WriteByte(s[i])
			i++
		}
	}
	return out.String()
}

func isANSIEscapeStart(s string, i int) bool {
	return s[i] == '\x1b' && i+1 < len(s) && s[i+1] == '['
}

func skipANSIEscape(s string, i int) int {
	for i < len(s) && !isANSITerminator(s[i]) {
		i++
	}
	if i < len(s) {
		i++
	}
	return i
}

func isANSITerminator(b byte) bool {
	return b >= 'A' && b <= 'z'
}

// --- Assembly builder ---

// Column layout: hold(8) + gap(1) + board(22) + gap(1) + right(8) = 40
func buildAssembly(rs game.RenderState, lines []string) {
	activeFP := map[game.Position]bool{}
	ghostFP := map[game.Position]bool{}
	var activePieceKind game.Kind
	if rs.Active != nil {
		activePieceKind = rs.Active.Kind
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Footprint {
			activeFP[p] = true
		}
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Ghost {
			ghostFP[p] = true
		}
	}

	const boardH = 22
	for row := 0; row < boardH; row++ {
		left := holdRow(rs, row)
		center := boardRow(rs, row, activeFP, ghostFP, activePieceKind)
		right := rightRow(rs, row)
		lines[row] = left + " " + center + " " + right
	}
}
package tui

import (
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// --- Hold panel (8 cols wide, rows 0..5 used; rows 6..21 blank) ---

func holdRow(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	switch row {
	case 0:
		return colChrome + "┌──────┐" + reset
	case 1:
		return colChrome + "│ HOLD │" + reset
	case 2, 3, 4:
		return previewPanelRow(rs.HeldKind, rs.HoldUsed, row-2)
	case 5:
		return colChrome + "└──────┘" + reset
	default:
		return "        " // 8 spaces
	}
}

// previewPanelRow renders one of the 3 interior rows of an 8-col panel,
// showing the piece preview in rows 0..1 (rows 2 is just padding).
// Panel interior is 6 chars (border │ + 6 + │ = 8).
func previewPanelRow(kind game.Kind, dimmed bool, previewRow int) string {
	if kind == game.KindNone {
		return colChrome + "│      │" + reset
	}
	cells := normalizedFootprint(kind)
	offsetCells := previewCenterOffset(cells)
	row3 := previewRowCells(cells, previewRow, offsetCells)

	var sb strings.Builder
	sb.WriteString(colChrome + "│" + reset)
	for _, filled := range row3 {
		sb.WriteString(previewCellStr(filled, dimmed, kind))
	}
	sb.WriteString(colChrome + "│" + reset)
	return sb.String()
}

func previewCenterOffset(cells []game.Position) int {
	maxCol := 0
	for _, p := range cells {
		if p.Col > maxCol {
			maxCol = p.Col
		}
	}
	return (3 - (maxCol + 1)) / 2
}

func previewRowCells(cells []game.Position, previewRow, offsetCells int) [3]bool {
	var row3 [3]bool
	for _, p := range cells {
		if p.Row == previewRow {
			c := p.Col + offsetCells
			if c >= 0 && c < 3 {
				row3[c] = true
			}
		}
	}
	return row3
}

func previewCellStr(filled, dimmed bool, kind game.Kind) string {
	if !filled {
		return "  "
	}
	if dimmed {
		return colFgDim + "██" + reset
	}
	return pieceColorActive(int(kind)) + "██" + reset
}

// normalizedFootprint returns piece cells in north rotation, 0-indexed from (0,0).
func normalizedFootprint(kind game.Kind) []game.Position {
	fp := game.Footprint(kind, game.Position{Col: 0, Row: 0}, game.RotationNorth)
	minC, minR := fp[0].Col, fp[0].Row
	for _, p := range fp {
		if p.Col < minC {
			minC = p.Col
		}
		if p.Row < minR {
			minR = p.Row
		}
	}
	norm := make([]game.Position, len(fp))
	for i, p := range fp {
		norm[i] = game.Position{Col: p.Col - minC, Row: p.Row - minR}
	}
	return norm
}

// --- Right panel (8 cols wide) ---

func rightRow(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	if row >= 2 && row <= 4 {
		return previewPanelRow(rs.NextKind, false, row-2)
	}
	return rightFixedRow(rs, row)
}

func rightFixedRow(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	switch row {
	case 0:
		return colChrome + "┌──────┐" + reset
	case 1:
		return colChrome + "│ NEXT │" + reset
	default:
		if row <= 14 {
			return rightStatRow(rs, row)
		}
		return "        " // 8 spaces
	}
}

func rightStatRow(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	if row <= 10 {
		return rightStatRowUpper(rs, row)
	}
	return rightStatRowLower(rs, row)
}

func rightStatRowUpper(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	if row <= 7 {
		return rightScoreBlock(rs, row)
	}
	return rightLevelBlock(rs, row)
}

func rightScoreBlock(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	switch row {
	case 5:
		return colChrome + "├──────┤" + reset
	case 6:
		return colChrome + "│  SCO │" + reset
	default: // row == 7
		return statValRow(clamp(rs.Score, 0, 999999), "%06d")
	}
}

func rightLevelBlock(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	switch row {
	case 8:
		return colChrome + "├──────┤" + reset
	case 9:
		return colChrome + "│  LVL │" + reset
	default: // row == 10
		return statValRowStr(fmt.Sprintf("%6s", fmt.Sprintf("%02d", clamp(rs.Level, 0, 99))))
	}
}

func rightStatRowLower(rs game.RenderState, row int) string {
	switch row {
	case 11:
		return colChrome + "├──────┤" + reset
	case 12:
		return colChrome + "│  LNS │" + reset
	case 13:
		return statValRowStr(fmt.Sprintf("%6s", fmt.Sprintf("%03d", clamp(rs.LinesClearedTotal, 0, 999))))
	default: // row == 14
		return colChrome + "└──────┘" + reset
	}
}

func statValRow(val int, fmtStr string) string {
	v := fmt.Sprintf(fmtStr, val)
	return colChrome + "│" + colFgPrim + v + colChrome + "│" + reset
}

func statValRowStr(v string) string {
	return colChrome + "│" + colFgPrim + v + colChrome + "│" + reset
}

func clamp(v, lo, hi int) int {
	if v < lo {
		return lo
	}
	if v > hi {
		return hi
	}
	return v
}

package game

import (
	"math/rand"
	"testing"
	"testing/quick"
)

// --- Rotation round-trip ---

func TestPropertyRotationRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(r uint8) bool {
		rot := Rotation(r % 4)
		return CCWRotation(CWRotation(rot)) == rot
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Bag always contains all 7 kinds after refill ---

func TestPropertyBagRefillContainsAll7Kinds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		b := NewBag(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		b.Refill()
		seen := make(map[Kind]bool, 7)
		for !b.Empty() {
			seen[b.Draw()] = true
		}
		return len(seen) == 7
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- GhostOrigin row >= active piece origin row ---

func TestPropertyGhostRowAtLeastOriginRow(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		// Clamp col to valid range so the piece can have a valid placement.
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: SpawnRow}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		ghost := ap.GhostOrigin(b)
		return ghost.Row >= ap.Origin.Row
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Board: full rows always inside [0, BoardRows) ---

func TestPropertyFullRowsAlwaysInBounds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		rng := rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed))
		b := NewBoard()
		// Randomly fill some cells.
		for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
			for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
				if rng.Intn(2) == 0 {
					b.SetCell(c, r, KindI)
				}
			}
		}
		for _, row := range b.FullRows() {
			if row < 0 || row >= BoardRows {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Board: clearing rows reduces total filled cells by exactly count*BoardCols ---

func TestPropertyClearRowsReducesCellCount(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		rng := rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed))
		b := NewBoard()
		// Fill all cols for a random subset of rows to guarantee full rows.
		var fullRowSet []int
		for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
			if rng.Intn(2) == 0 {
				for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
					b.SetCell(c, r, KindI)
				}
				fullRowSet = append(fullRowSet, r)
			}
		}
		if len(fullRowSet) == 0 {
			return true // nothing to clear
		}
		before := len(b.FilledCells())
		b.ClearRows(fullRowSet)
		after := len(b.FilledCells())
		return before-after == len(fullRowSet)*BoardCols
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Score: hard drop never decreases score ---

func TestPropertyHardDropNeverDecreasesScore(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		before := s.Score
		s.PlayerHardDrops()
		return s.Score >= before
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Level: level is always >= 1 ---

func TestPropertyLevelAlwaysAtLeastOne(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64, drops uint8) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		n := int(drops) % 20
		for i := 0; i < n && s.Phase == PhasePlaying; i++ {
			s.PlayerHardDrops()
		}
		return s.Level >= 1
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Footprint: all offsets for a piece stay within a 4x4 bounding box ---

func TestPropertyFootprintWithin4x4(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		origin := Position{Col: 3, Row: 3}
		for _, p := range Footprint(k, origin, r) {
			dc := p.Col - origin.Col
			dr := p.Row - origin.Row
			if dc < 0 || dc > 3 || dr < 0 || dr > 3 {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Snapshot: waiting phase has no active piece ---

func TestPropertySnapshotWaitingHasNoActive(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		rs := s.Snapshot()
		return rs.Phase == PhaseWaiting && rs.Active == nil
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Snapshot: active footprint matches session active piece ---

func TestPropertySnapshotActiveMirrorsSession(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
			return true
		}
		rs := s.Snapshot()
		if rs.Active == nil {
			return false
		}
		want := s.ActivePiece.Footprint()
		if len(rs.Active.Footprint) != len(want) {
			return false
		}
		got := make(map[Position]bool, len(rs.Active.Footprint))
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Footprint {
			got[p] = true
		}
		for _, p := range want {
			if !got[p] {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Snapshot: board grid matches Board.Cell for all visible cells ---

func TestPropertySnapshotBoardConsistency(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64, drops uint8) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		n := int(drops) % 10
		for i := 0; i < n && s.Phase == PhasePlaying; i++ {
			s.PlayerHardDrops()
		}
		if s.Board == nil {
			return true
		}
		rs := s.Snapshot()
		for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
			for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
				if rs.Board[r][c] != s.Board.Cell(c, r) {
					return false
				}
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Snapshot: ghost positions do not overlap active footprint ---

func TestPropertySnapshotGhostNoOverlapActive(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		rs := s.Snapshot()
		if rs.Active == nil {
			return true
		}
		fpSet := make(map[Position]bool, len(rs.Active.Footprint))
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Footprint {
			fpSet[p] = true
		}
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Ghost {
			if fpSet[p] {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- LockTicksRemaining: move resets lock timer to LockTicks ---

func TestPropertyMoveResetsLockTimer(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
			return true
		}
		// Drain most of the lock counter by ticking until piece is at rest.
		for i := 0; i < 50 && s.Phase == PhasePlaying; i++ {
			s.Tick()
			if s.ActivePiece != nil && s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
				break
			}
		}
		if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
			return true
		}
		if !s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
			return true // piece not at rest, invariant doesn't apply
		}
		// Partially drain lock timer.
		if s.LockTicksRemaining > 1 {
			s.Tick()
		}
		pre := s.LockTicksRemaining
		// A successful move must reset LockTicksRemaining to LockTicks.
		moved := s.PlayerMovesLeft() || s.PlayerMovesRight()
		if !moved {
			return true // no move possible; invariant not triggered
		}
		return s.LockTicksRemaining == LockTicks || s.LockTicksRemaining > pre
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- LockTicksRemaining: always in range [1, LockTicks] during play ---

func TestPropertyLockTicksInRange(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		s.PlayerStartsGame()
		for i := 0; i < 30 && s.Phase == PhasePlaying; i++ {
			if s.LockTicksRemaining < 1 || s.LockTicksRemaining > LockTicks {
				return false
			}
			s.Tick()
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Rotation: successful CW placement always within column bounds ---

func TestPropertyRotateCWFootprintInBounds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: 5}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		newOrigin, newRot, ok := ap.TryRotateCW(b)
		if !ok {
			return true // blocked — no placement to check
		}
		for _, p := range Footprint(k, newOrigin, newRot) {
			if p.Col < 0 || p.Col >= BoardCols {
				return false
			}
			if p.Row >= BoardRows {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Rotation: successful CCW placement always within column bounds ---

func TestPropertyRotateCCWFootprintInBounds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: 5}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		newOrigin, newRot, ok := ap.TryRotateCCW(b)
		if !ok {
			return true
		}
		for _, p := range Footprint(k, newOrigin, newRot) {
			if p.Col < 0 || p.Col >= BoardCols {
				return false
			}
			if p.Row >= BoardRows {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Rotation: successful rotation always yields a valid placement ---

func TestPropertyRotateCWYieldsValidPlacement(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: 5}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		newOrigin, newRot, ok := ap.TryRotateCW(b)
		if !ok {
			return true
		}
		result := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: newOrigin, Rotation: newRot}
		return result.ValidPlacement(b)
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Rotation: successful CCW rotation always yields a valid placement ---

func TestPropertyRotateCCWYieldsValidPlacement(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: 5}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		newOrigin, newRot, ok := ap.TryRotateCCW(b)
		if !ok {
			return true
		}
		result := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: newOrigin, Rotation: newRot}
		return result.ValidPlacement(b)
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Rotation: CanRotateCW/CCW consistent with TryRotateCW/CCW ---

func TestPropertyCanRotateConsistentWithTry(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: 5}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		_, _, okCW := ap.TryRotateCW(b)
		_, _, okCCW := ap.TryRotateCCW(b)
		return ap.CanRotateCW(b) == okCW && ap.CanRotateCCW(b) == okCCW
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

package tui

import (
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"testing/quick"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// Property tests for the flash rendering invariants in playingCell.
// These are kept separate from unit/acceptance tests per engineering rules.

// boardVocab is the set of 2-char glyphs the board may produce (plain, ANSI-stripped).
var boardVocab = []string{"██", "░░", "· ", "─ ", "┌─", "└─"}

// TestPropertyFlashRowAlwaysRendersBlock verifies that any position in a flash row
// always renders as "██" regardless of board state or active/ghost positions.
func TestPropertyFlashRowAlwaysRendersBlock(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(rowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row}

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{row},
		}
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		return cell == "██"
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyFlashRowPriorityOverActive verifies that a flash row overrides
// an active piece cell — flash rendering takes highest priority.
func TestPropertyFlashRowPriorityOverActive(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(rowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8, kindIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row}
		kind := game.AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%len(game.AllKinds)]

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{row},
		}
		// pos is both in flash row AND in active footprint
		activeFP := map[game.Position]bool{pos: true}
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, activeFP, map[game.Position]bool{}, kind))
		return cell == "██"
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyFlashRowPriorityOverGhost verifies that a flash row overrides
// a ghost cell.
func TestPropertyFlashRowPriorityOverGhost(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(rowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8, kindIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row}
		kind := game.AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%len(game.AllKinds)]

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{row},
		}
		// pos is in flash row AND ghost
		ghostFP := map[game.Position]bool{pos: true}
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, map[game.Position]bool{}, ghostFP, kind))
		return cell == "██"
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyNonFlashRowNeverRendersFlashGlyph verifies that a position NOT in
// any flash row never produces the flash-white "██" from the flash branch.
// (Active and locked cells also produce ██ by design; we verify the flash path
// doesn't activate for rows not listed in FlashRows.)
func TestPropertyNonFlashRowNotTriggeredByFlash(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(flashRowIdx uint8, testRowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		flashRow := int(flashRowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		testRow := int(testRowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		if testRow == flashRow {
			return true // skip overlapping case
		}
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: testRow}

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{flashRow},
		}
		// empty active/ghost and no locked cell — should render as "· " (empty)
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		return cell == "· "
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyPlayingCellCharsetBounded verifies every cell in a full board row
// is bounded to the allowed 2-char glyph vocabulary: ██, ░░, or · .
func TestPropertyPlayingCellCharsetBounded(t *testing.T) {
	allowed := map[string]bool{
		"██": true,
		"░░": true,
		"· ": true,
	}
	f := func(rowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row}

		rs := game.RenderState{Phase: game.PhasePlaying}
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		return allowed[cell]
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyFlashAndNonFlashNeverCoRendered verifies that when FlashRows is set,
// a position in a flash row renders as flash (white) while a position NOT in the
// flash row renders normally — the two states are mutually exclusive within the
// same frame.
func TestPropertyFlashAndNonFlashMutuallyExclusive(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(flashRowIdx uint8, nonFlashRowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		flashRow := int(flashRowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		nonFlashRow := int(nonFlashRowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		if flashRow == nonFlashRow {
			return true
		}
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{flashRow},
		}

		flashPos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: flashRow}
		nonFlashPos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: nonFlashRow}

		flashCell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, flashPos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		nonFlashCell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, nonFlashPos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))

		// flash row must render ██; non-flash empty row must render ·
		flashOK := flashCell == "██"
		nonFlashOK := nonFlashCell == "· "
		return flashOK && nonFlashOK
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyBoardRowAlwaysLength22 verifies boardRow always returns a string
// that renders to exactly 22 display columns (borders + 10 cells × 2 chars).
func TestPropertyBoardRowAlwaysLength22(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(rowIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % 22) // valid assembly rows 0..21
		rs := game.RenderState{Phase: game.PhasePlaying}
		plain := stripANSI(boardRow(rs, row, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		// Count rune display width
		w := 0
		for range plain {
			w++
		}
		return w == 22
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("boardRow display width not 22: %v", err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyMultipleFlashRowsAllRenderBlock verifies that when multiple rows
// are listed in FlashRows, each row position renders as "██".
func TestPropertyMultipleFlashRowsAllRenderBlock(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(r0 uint8, r1 uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		row0 := int(r0 % game.BoardRows)
		row1 := int(r1 % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: []int{row0, row1},
		}

		pos0 := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row0}
		pos1 := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row1}

		cell0 := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos0, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		cell1 := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos1, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		return cell0 == "██" && cell1 == "██"
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// TestPropertyEmptyFlashRowsNeverRendersFlash verifies that when FlashRows is
// empty/nil, no cell renders via the flash path (the empty board renders "· ").
func TestPropertyEmptyFlashRowsNeverRendersFlash(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(rowIdx uint8, colIdx uint8) bool {
		row := int(rowIdx % game.BoardRows)
		col := int(colIdx % game.BoardCols)
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: row}

		rs := game.RenderState{
			Phase:     game.PhasePlaying,
			FlashRows: nil,
		}
		cell := stripANSI(playingCell(rs, pos, map[game.Position]bool{}, map[game.Position]bool{}, game.KindNone))
		return strings.Contains("██░░· ", cell)
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

package tui

import (
	"time"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// RunConfig holds optional overrides for the game loop (used for QA and testing).
type RunConfig struct {
	Seed              int64 // 0 = random
	TickRateMs        int   // 0 = default (100ms)
	PrefillRows       int   // fill this many bottom rows with locked cells (leaving PrefillGapCol empty)
	PrefillGapCol     int   // column left empty when PrefillRows > 0 (default 9)
	InitLinesClearedN int   // pre-set LinesClearedTotal and Level on game start (for QA level scenarios)
}

// tickIntervalFor returns the tick duration to use for the given config.
func tickIntervalFor(cfg RunConfig) time.Duration {
	if cfg.TickRateMs > 0 {
		return time.Duration(cfg.TickRateMs) * time.Millisecond
	}
	return time.Second / defaultTickHz
}

// applyQASetup applies prefill and init-lines setup on the first time the game enters playing phase.
// Reports whether setup was applied (so caller can stop calling after the first application).
func applyQASetup(s *game.Session, cfg RunConfig) bool {
	if s.CurrentPhase() != game.PhasePlaying {
		return false
	}
	if cfg.PrefillRows > 0 {
		applyPrefill(s, cfg.PrefillRows, cfg.PrefillGapCol)
	}
	if cfg.InitLinesClearedN > 0 {
		applyInitLines(s, cfg.InitLinesClearedN)
	}
	return true
}

// selectFrame returns the appropriate frame for the given terminal dimensions and session state.
func selectFrame(s *game.Session, w, h int) Frame {
	if w < minCols || h < minRows {
		return RenderTooSmall(w, h)
	}
	return Render(s.Snapshot(), w, h)
}

// isTooSmall reports whether the given terminal dimensions are below the minimum.
func isTooSmall(w, h int) bool {
	return w < minCols || h < minRows
}

// applyInitLines pre-sets LinesClearedTotal and Level to match n lines already cleared.
func applyInitLines(s *game.Session, n int) {
	if n <= 0 || s.Board == nil {
		return
	}
	s.LinesClearedTotal = n
	s.Level = n/10 + 1
}

// applyPrefill fills the bottom n rows with locked cells, leaving gapCol empty in each row.
func applyPrefill(s *game.Session, rows, gapCol int) {
	if rows <= 0 || s.Board == nil {
		return
	}
	for row := game.BoardRows - rows; row < game.BoardRows; row++ {
		for col := 0; col < game.BoardCols; col++ {
			if col != gapCol {
				s.Board.SetCell(col, row, game.KindI)
			}
		}
	}
}

package game

// SpawnBufferRows is the number of hidden rows above the visible board.
// Pieces spawn at row SpawnRow = -2, so the buffer must cover rows -2..-1.
const SpawnBufferRows = 2

// totalRows is the full internal storage height: buffer + visible.
const totalRows = SpawnBufferRows + BoardRows

// Board is the 10×20 visible play field plus a 2-row hidden spawn buffer.
// Internal storage uses rows 0..totalRows-1; public API uses row indices
// where 0 is the topmost visible row and negative indices address the buffer.
type Board struct {
	// cells[internalRow][col]; internalRow = row + SpawnBufferRows
	cells [totalRows][BoardCols]Kind
}

// NewBoard returns a blank board.
func NewBoard() *Board {
	return &Board{}
}

func (b *Board) toInternal(row int) int {
	return row + SpawnBufferRows
}

// Cell returns the Kind at (col, row). Returns KindNone for out-of-range rows.
func (b *Board) Cell(col, row int) Kind {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 || ir >= totalRows {
		return KindNone
	}
	return b.cells[ir][col]
}

// SetCell places a kind at (col, row). No-op for out-of-range rows.
func (b *Board) SetCell(col, row int, k Kind) {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 || ir >= totalRows {
		return
	}
	b.cells[ir][col] = k
}

// CellOccupied reports whether (col, row) contains a locked cell.
// Returns true for column out-of-bounds or row below the bottom.
// Returns false for rows above the buffer top (shouldn't occur in play).
func (b *Board) CellOccupied(col, row int) bool {
	if col < 0 || col >= BoardCols {
		return true
	}
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 {
		return false
	}
	if ir >= totalRows {
		return true
	}
	return b.cells[ir][col] != KindNone
}

// FullRows returns visible row indices (0-indexed) where every cell is filled.
func (b *Board) FullRows() []int {
	var full []int
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		if b.isRowFull(r) {
			full = append(full, r)
		}
	}
	return full
}

func (b *Board) isRowFull(row int) bool {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
		if b.cells[ir][c] == KindNone {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// ClearRows removes the given visible rows and drops everything above them.
// Rows must be provided in ascending order.
func (b *Board) ClearRows(rows []int) {
	if len(rows) == 0 {
		return
	}
	cleared := make(map[int]bool, len(rows))
	for _, r := range rows {
		cleared[r] = true
	}

	// Rebuild internal storage from bottom up, skipping cleared rows.
	var next [totalRows][BoardCols]Kind
	dst := totalRows - 1
	for src := totalRows - 1; src >= 0; src-- {
		visibleRow := src - SpawnBufferRows
		if cleared[visibleRow] {
			continue
		}
		next[dst] = b.cells[src]
		dst--
	}
	b.cells = next
}

// FilledCells returns all visible (col, row, kind) tuples where kind != KindNone.
func (b *Board) FilledCells() []struct {
	Col, Row int
	Kind     Kind
} {
	var out []struct {
		Col, Row int
		Kind     Kind
	}
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		ir := b.toInternal(r)
		for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
			if b.cells[ir][c] != KindNone {
				out = append(out, struct {
					Col, Row int
					Kind     Kind
				}{c, r, b.cells[ir][c]})
			}
		}
	}
	return out
}

package game

// Footprint returns the absolute board positions for kind at origin+rotation.
// k must be KindI..KindZ (not KindNone).
func Footprint(k Kind, origin Position, rot Rotation) []Position {
	offsets := pieceFootprints[k-1][rot]
	out := make([]Position, len(offsets))
	for i, o := range offsets {
		out[i] = Position{Col: origin.Col + o.Col, Row: origin.Row + o.Row}
	}
	return out
}

// ActivePiece is the falling piece currently controlled by the player.
type ActivePiece struct {
	Kind     Kind
	Origin   Position
	Rotation Rotation
}

// Footprint returns the absolute board positions of this piece.
func (ap *ActivePiece) Footprint() []Position {
	return Footprint(ap.Kind, ap.Origin, ap.Rotation)
}

// ValidPlacement reports whether all footprint cells are in-column-bounds and
// not occupied on the board, including the hidden spawn buffer rows.
func (ap *ActivePiece) ValidPlacement(b *Board) bool {
	for _, p := range ap.Footprint() {
		if b.CellOccupied(p.Col, p.Row) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// AtRest reports whether shifting origin.row by +1 would make ValidPlacement false.
func (ap *ActivePiece) AtRest(b *Board) bool {
	below := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col, Row: ap.Origin.Row + 1}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return !below.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// GhostOrigin returns the lowest row at which the piece still has a valid placement.
func (ap *ActivePiece) GhostOrigin(b *Board) Position {
	ghost := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: ap.Origin, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	for {
		next := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ghost.Origin.Col, Row: ghost.Origin.Row + 1}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
		if !next.ValidPlacement(b) {
			break
		}
		ghost.Origin = next.Origin
	}
	return ghost.Origin
}

// CanMoveLeft reports whether moving one column left is valid.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanMoveLeft(b *Board) bool {
	moved := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col - 1, Row: ap.Origin.Row}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return moved.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// CanMoveRight reports whether moving one column right is valid.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanMoveRight(b *Board) bool {
	moved := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col + 1, Row: ap.Origin.Row}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return moved.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// CWRotation returns the next clockwise rotation state.
func CWRotation(r Rotation) Rotation {
	return (r + 1) % 4
}

// CCWRotation returns the next counter-clockwise rotation state.
func CCWRotation(r Rotation) Rotation {
	return (r + 3) % 4
}

// kickTable returns the appropriate SRS kick table for this piece kind.
func kickTable(k Kind) *[4][4][5]Position {
	if k == KindI {
		return &srsKicksI
	}
	return &srsKicksJLSTZ
}

// TryRotateCW attempts a CW rotation using SRS kicks.
// Returns (newOrigin, newRotation, true) if a valid kick is found, or (_, _, false) if blocked.
func (ap *ActivePiece) TryRotateCW(b *Board) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	return ap.tryRotate(b, CWRotation(ap.Rotation))
}

// TryRotateCCW attempts a CCW rotation using SRS kicks.
// Returns (newOrigin, newRotation, true) if a valid kick is found, or (_, _, false) if blocked.
func (ap *ActivePiece) TryRotateCCW(b *Board) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	return ap.tryRotate(b, CCWRotation(ap.Rotation))
}

// CanRotateCW reports whether a CW rotation has at least one valid SRS kick.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanRotateCW(b *Board) bool {
	_, _, ok := ap.TryRotateCW(b)
	return ok
}

// CanRotateCCW reports whether a CCW rotation has at least one valid SRS kick.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanRotateCCW(b *Board) bool {
	_, _, ok := ap.TryRotateCCW(b)
	return ok
}

func (ap *ActivePiece) tryRotate(b *Board, toRot Rotation) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	kicks := kickTable(ap.Kind)
	for _, offset := range kicks[ap.Rotation][toRot] {
		candidate := &ActivePiece{
			Kind:     ap.Kind,
			Origin:   Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col + offset.Col, Row: ap.Origin.Row + offset.Row},
			Rotation: toRot,
		}
		if candidate.ValidPlacement(b) {
			return candidate.Origin, toRot, true
		}
	}
	return Position{}, toRot, false
}

package game

// Test fixture methods for acceptance and integration tests.
// These provide controlled state injection without requiring direct field access,
// so acceptance tests in external packages don't depend on Session's field layout.

// SetActivePieceForTest places a specific piece as the active piece.
// Only valid when phase == playing or paused.
func (s *Session) SetActivePieceForTest(kind Kind, col, row int, rot Rotation) {
	s.ActivePiece = &ActivePiece{
		Kind:     kind,
		Origin:   Position{Col: col, Row: row},
		Rotation: rot,
	}
}

// SetActivePosForTest repositions the active piece without changing kind or rotation.
// No-op if ActivePiece is nil.
func (s *Session) SetActivePosForTest(col, row int) {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = Position{Col: col, Row: row}
}

// ClearActivePieceForTest sets the active piece to nil.
// Used to construct game-over setup without going through normal piece locking.
func (s *Session) ClearActivePieceForTest() {
	s.ActivePiece = nil
}

// SetBoardCellForTest sets a single cell on the board.
// Only valid when Board is non-nil (i.e. phase == playing or paused).
func (s *Session) SetBoardCellForTest(col, row int, k Kind) {
	if s.Board == nil {
		return
	}
	s.Board.SetCell(col, row, k)
}

// SetScoreForTest overrides the session score.
func (s *Session) SetScoreForTest(score int) {
	s.Score = score
}

// SetLevelForTest overrides the session level and total lines cleared.
func (s *Session) SetLevelForTest(level, linesClearedTotal int) {
	s.Level = level
	s.LinesClearedTotal = linesClearedTotal
}

// ActiveOriginForTest returns the active piece's origin, or Position{} if none.
func (s *Session) ActiveOriginForTest() Position {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return Position{}
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.Origin
}

// ActiveRotationForTest returns the active piece's rotation, or RotationNorth if none.
func (s *Session) ActiveRotationForTest() Rotation {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return RotationNorth
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.Rotation
}

// ActiveKindForTest returns the active piece's kind, or KindNone if none.
func (s *Session) ActiveKindForTest() Kind {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return KindNone
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.Kind
}

// ActivePieceNilForTest reports whether there is no active piece.
func (s *Session) ActivePieceNilForTest() bool {
	return s.ActivePiece == nil
}

// ActiveCanMoveLeftForTest reports whether the active piece can move left.
func (s *Session) ActiveCanMoveLeftForTest() bool {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return false
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.CanMoveLeft(s.Board)
}

// ActiveCanMoveRightForTest reports whether the active piece can move right.
func (s *Session) ActiveCanMoveRightForTest() bool {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return false
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.CanMoveRight(s.Board)
}

// ActiveCanRotateCWForTest reports whether the active piece can rotate clockwise.
func (s *Session) ActiveCanRotateCWForTest() bool {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return false
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.CanRotateCW(s.Board)
}

// ActiveCanRotateCCWForTest reports whether the active piece can rotate counter-clockwise.
func (s *Session) ActiveCanRotateCCWForTest() bool {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return false
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.CanRotateCCW(s.Board)
}

// ActiveGhostOriginForTest returns the ghost origin for the active piece.
func (s *Session) ActiveGhostOriginForTest() Position {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return Position{}
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
}

// ActiveAtRestForTest reports whether the active piece is at rest.
func (s *Session) ActiveAtRestForTest() bool {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {
		return false
	}
	return s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board)
}

// BoardCellForTest reads a single cell from the board.
func (s *Session) BoardCellForTest(col, row int) Kind {
	if s.Board == nil {
		return KindNone
	}
	return s.Board.Cell(col, row)
}

// ScoreForTest returns the session score.
func (s *Session) ScoreForTest() int {
	return s.Score
}

// LevelForTest returns the session level.
func (s *Session) LevelForTest() int {
	return s.Level
}

// LinesClearedTotalForTest returns the total lines cleared.
func (s *Session) LinesClearedTotalForTest() int {
	return s.LinesClearedTotal
}

// SetLockTicksRemainingForTest overrides the lock countdown.
func (s *Session) SetLockTicksRemainingForTest(ticks int) {
	s.LockTicksRemaining = ticks
}

// LockTicksRemainingForTest returns the current lock countdown.
func (s *Session) LockTicksRemainingForTest() int {
	return s.LockTicksRemaining
}

// BagRemainingForTest returns the piece kinds remaining in the bag.
func (s *Session) BagRemainingForTest() []Kind {
	if s.Bag == nil {
		return nil
	}
	return s.Bag.Remaining()
}

// DrainBagForTest drains the bag for test setup.
func (s *Session) DrainBagForTest() {
	if s.Bag != nil {
		s.Bag.DrainForTest()
	}
}

// HeldKindForTest returns the held piece kind.
func (s *Session) HeldKindForTest() Kind {
	return s.HeldKind
}

// HoldUsedForTest reports whether hold has been used this piece.
func (s *Session) HoldUsedForTest() bool {
	return s.HoldUsed
}

// SetHeldKindForTest sets the held piece kind for test setup.
func (s *Session) SetHeldKindForTest(k Kind) {
	s.HeldKind = k
}

// SetHoldUsedForTest sets hold_used for test setup.
func (s *Session) SetHoldUsedForTest(used bool) {
	s.HoldUsed = used
}

This violates the boundary that the fixture methods were created to enforce.

Additionally, I need to check: does `arch_test.go` only enforce `game → tui` direction, or also `tui → game.internal`? The current arch test only prevents `game` from importing `tui`. But the tui code using `s.Board.SetCell()` directly (bypassing `s.SetBoardCellForTest()`) and `s.Phase` directly is the key violation.

> AGENT

Now I have a clear picture. The architectural violations are:

1. **`pkg/tui/setup.go`** directly accesses `s.Board.SetCell()` and `s.Board == nil`, `s.LinesClearedTotal`, `s.Level` — bypassing `SetBoardCellForTest`, `SetLevelForTest`. This is production code, not just tests.

2. **`pkg/tui` test files** directly access `s.Phase`, `s.ActivePiece.*`, `s.Board.*`, `s.HeldKind`, `s.HoldUsed` — bypassing `ForTest` accessor methods.

The key issue for production code is `setup.go` — it directly mutates Session fields via `s.Board.SetCell()`, `s.LinesClearedTotal`, `s.Level`. These should use the fixture/test API. However, looking carefully: `setup.go` is QA/testing infrastructure within the tui package itself (it's for `RunConfig`-driven QA setup). Now I understand the full set of fixes needed:

**For `pkg/tui/setup.go` (production code):**
1. `applyInitLines`: replace direct `s.LinesClearedTotal = n` / `s.Level = ...` with `s.SetLevelForTest(n/10+1, n)`
2. `applyInitLines`: replace `s.Board == nil` guard with a check via `s.CurrentPhase()`
3. `applyPrefill`: replace `s.Board.SetCell(...)` with `s.SetBoardCellForTest(...)`  
4. `applyPrefill`: replace `s.Board == nil` guard with `s.CurrentPhase()` check

**For test files** (`dispatch_test.go`, `qa_suite_test.go`, etc.): These already bypass the fixture API, but test files are harder — the arch test only checks the production code. The existing `arch_test.go` already enforces direction for `game`. 

However, the main architectural concern is `setup.go` (production/runtime code) touching internals. The test files are a secondary concern — test code accessing test-fixture methods is acceptable and the `ForTest` methods exist precisely for that use.

1	package tui
2	
3	import (
4		"time"
5	
6		"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
7	)
8	
9	// RunConfig holds optional overrides for the game loop (used for QA and testing).
10	type RunConfig struct {
11		Seed              int64 // 0 = random
12		TickRateMs        int   // 0 = default (100ms)
13		PrefillRows       int   // fill this many bottom rows with locked cells (leaving PrefillGapCol empty)
14		PrefillGapCol     int   // column left empty when PrefillRows > 0 (default 9)
15		InitLinesClearedN int   // pre-set LinesClearedTotal and Level on game start (for QA level scenarios)
16	}
17	
18	// tickIntervalFor returns the tick duration to use for the given config.
19	func tickIntervalFor(cfg RunConfig) time.Duration {
20		if cfg.TickRateMs > 0 {
21			return time.Duration(cfg.TickRateMs) * time.Millisecond
22		}
23		return time.Second / defaultTickHz
24	}
25	
26	// applyQASetup applies prefill and init-lines setup on the first time the game enters playing phase.
27	// Reports whether setup was applied (so caller can stop calling after the first application).
28	func applyQASetup(s *game.Session, cfg RunConfig) bool {
29		if s.CurrentPhase() != game.PhasePlaying {
30			return false
31		}
32		if cfg.PrefillRows > 0 {
33			applyPrefill(s, cfg.PrefillRows, cfg.PrefillGapCol)
34		}
35		if cfg.InitLinesClearedN > 0 {
36			applyInitLines(s, cfg.InitLinesClearedN)
37		}
38		return true
39	}
40	
41	// selectFrame returns the appropriate frame for the given terminal dimensions and session state.
42	func selectFrame(s *game.Session, w, h int) Frame {
43		if w < minCols || h < minRows {
44			return RenderTooSmall(w, h)
45		}
46		return Render(s.Snapshot(), w, h)
47	}
48	
49	// isTooSmall reports whether the given terminal dimensions are below the minimum.
50	func isTooSmall(w, h int) bool {
51		return w < minCols || h < minRows
52	}
53	
54	// applyInitLines pre-sets LinesClearedTotal and Level to match n lines already cleared.
55	func applyInitLines(s *game.Session, n int) {
56		if n <= 0 || s.Board == nil {
57			return
58		}
59		s.LinesClearedTotal = n
60		s.Level = n/10 + 1
61	}
62	
63	// applyPrefill fills the bottom n rows with locked cells, leaving gapCol empty in each row.
64	func applyPrefill(s *game.Session, rows, gapCol int) {
65		if rows <= 0 || s.Board == nil {
66			return
67		}
68		for row := game.BoardRows - rows; row < game.BoardRows; row++ {
69			for col := 0; col < game.BoardCols; col++ {
70				if col != gapCol {
71					s.Board.SetCell(col, row, game.KindI)
72				}
73			}
74		}
75	}
76	
```

> AGENT

The `Phase` field doesn't have a `ForTest` method — `CurrentPhase()` is the public accessor. However, the test files use `s.Phase` directly as a value comparison, which is fine since `CurrentPhase()` exists. Similarly `HoldUsedForTest()` and `HeldKindForTest()` exist.

Now there's an important question: `Phase` is a public field (capital P), so test code accessing it directly is technically allowed in Go. The architectural concern is that `Session` has many exported fields, meaning the entire internal layout is part of its public API surface. The cleaner future direction would be to unexport fields and only expose them via the accessor/snapshot pattern — but that's a bigger refactor.

For now, the critical boundary violation to fix architecturally is:
1. **`setup.go`** (done — uses `SetBoardCellForTest` and `SetLevelForTest`)
2. Test files should prefer `CurrentPhase()` over `s.Phase`, and use `ForTest` methods over direct field access

16	// TestHandleKeyTooSmall verifies that tooSmall=true suppresses all input.
17	func TestHandleKeyTooSmall(t *testing.T) {
18		s := playingSession()
19		before := s.ActivePiece.Origin
20		handleKey(s, KeyLeft, true)
21		if s.ActivePiece.Origin != before {

29		if s.Phase != game.PhaseWaiting {
30			t.Fatal("session must start in waiting phase")
31		}
32		handleKey(s, KeyEnter, false)
33		if s.Phase != game.PhasePlaying {
34			t.Errorf("handleKey(KeyEnter) in waiting: phase = %v, want playing", s.Phase)
